Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, well, I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble with... with my eating habits. I hope it's okay to bring this up, I feel a little embarrassed even saying it out loud. 

But, um, what really scares me is that sometimes, once I start eating—especially if I'm just alone in the kitchen—it almost feels like... like I’ve stepped aside? Um... no, not really. I guess that's exactly what I meant a second ago when I said it feels like I've just... stepped aside.

I guess that's exactly what I meant a second ago when I said it feels like I've just... stepped aside. \n\nLike I was saying, I'll literally be standing there, completely aware, and I'll be in my own head saying, 'Janet, you really need to put it away now.' I mean, it's just food, I know I should be able to just walk away from the counter, right? Um... well, I suppose it depends on what you'd call a large amount? I really try not to be gluttonous, I promise. But... well, on some afternoons, like if I've just finished a shift at the food bank, or if I've been baking for a senior-center event... I'll come home and start with a full plate of leftovers. I guess that's exactly what I meant a second ago when I said it feels like I've just... stepped aside. \n\nLike I was saying, I'll literally be standing there, completely aware, and I'll be in my own head saying, 'Janet, you really need to put it away now.' But my hands just keep going. I just can't seem to make the physical action stop. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds so ridiculous to say out loud. I mean, it's just food, I know I should be able to just walk away from the counter, right? But in those moments, I really don't feel like I'm the one steering the ship, if that makes sense? \n\nI hope I'm answering your question okay. I just... I don't want to sound dramatic, but no, the control just isn't there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh... um. Yes. You really hit the nail on the head with that one. \n\nI... well, I feel just awful about myself afterward. I mean, it's a little silly, right? I'm 48 years old, I really feel like I'm way too old to be acting this way. It's just so embarrassing to admit to you. I'll look at the kitchen and realize I've wasted food that I actually meant to save and share with everyone else. And then I just... I usually just sit there feeling so ashamed. I always promise myself that it absolutely won't happen again, and I really do try to be better about it. \n\nI hope I'm not sounding too pathetic complaining about this... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, let me think. I guess if I really look back at my kitchen calendar and, you know, the weeks I go to the food bank... it's been happening most weeks. Maybe just once or twice a week, usually? And it's been going on for a good few months now, I suppose. \n\nI mean, it's not every single day, so I hope that means it's not too terrible? I really am trying my best to keep it under control. Does that sound like a lot to you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you feel like you've lost control, do you typically eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it depends on what you'd call a large amount? I really try not to be gluttonous, I promise. But... well, on some afternoons, like if I've just finished a shift at the food bank, or if I've been baking for a senior-center event... I'll come home and start with a full plate of leftovers. Which isn't too bad on its own, right? \n\nBut then... then I'll just keep going. I'll have a few slices of banana bread, and some chips, and cheese, and... and just whatever cookies happen to be in the tin. \n\nAnd afterward, when I actually look at what I did, I realize it was way, way more than any meal I’d ever serve to another person. It's hard to admit that. I hope I'm not sounding too awful saying all that out loud... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I would never do anything like that. I know some people do, and I'm not judging them, but... no, I don't make myself sick or take any of those pills. And as for exercising, well, I try to stay active by walking around the neighborhood and keeping busy at the senior center, but I certainly don't overdo it. \n\nI just... I just feel bad about it and try to promise myself I'll do better next time. I hope that's the right answer? Oh... um, well... yes. I do. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not healthy, but... it's hard not to feel just awful afterward.

I sit there and think, I'm forty-eight years old, I'm far too old to be acting this way. I should know better by now. I always promise myself it won't happen again, and I really mean it when I say it. I just want to do better. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too terribly dramatic.

I'll have a few slices of banana bread, and some chips, and cheese, and... and just whatever cookies happen to be in the tin. \n\nAnd afterward, when I actually look at what I did, I realize it was way, way more than any meal I’d ever serve to another person. It's hard to admit that. I hope I'm not sounding too awful saying all that out loud... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I would never do anything like that. I know some people do, and I'm not judging them, but... no, I don't make myself sick or take any of those pills. And as for exercising, well, I try to stay active by walking around the neighborhood and keeping busy at the senior center, but I certainly don't overdo it. \n\nI just... I just feel bad about it and try to promise myself I'll do better next time. I hope that's the right answer? I mean, I know the eating part is bad enough, but I really do try to be sensible otherwise. Is that what you were hoping to hear?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those times when you can't seem to stop?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I try to be mindful, I really do, but... sometimes I'll just catch myself eating so fast. Like, I'll be standing right there at the kitchen counter with the casserole or the muffins I just baked, and I'm just taking bite after bite. \n\nI barely even taste it when I get like that. It's almost like I'm in this terrible hurry, even though I have absolutely nowhere I need to be. It's so silly, isn't it? I know I should sit down and use a plate properly like a normal person. I really am trying to work on that. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control happen at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose they do. When I really sit down and look at my kitchen calendar, or think about my shifts at the food bank... it's been happening most weeks. Usually once, maybe twice a week. And it's been going on like that for... well, for many months now, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I really have been trying so hard to get it under control. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to be healthy. It's just... it slips away from me sometimes. Is that... is that the kind of timeline you needed to know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you accurate information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I mean, my weight goes up and down a little bit, like anyone else's, I suppose. Maybe a few pounds here or there, but nothing I would call significant. \n\nI try to wear clothes that are comfortable and flattering, and they all still fit just fine. I really do try to keep an eye on it, you know? I don't want to let myself go. I hope that's alright? I mean, I know with the... the eating things I mentioned, you might expect a big change, but I really have managed to keep it mostly steady. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself after these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... yes. I do. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not healthy, but... it's hard not to feel just awful afterward. \n\nI'll look at the empty pan and just think about how I've wasted food that I really meant to share with others, you know? And I just feel so ashamed. I sit there and think, I'm forty-eight years old, I'm far too old to be acting this way. I should know better by now. I always promise myself it won't happen again, and I really mean it when I say it. I just want to do better. Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too terribly dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize your body shape or weight after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really about my body shape or my weight. I mean, I know I just said I feel awful, but it's more about the act itself, you know? The... the loss of control and the waste. \n\nI try very hard to appreciate my body for what it can do. I walk every day and I stay active with my volunteering. So when I get upset with myself, it's not really about how I look or a number on a scale. It's more just... disappointment in my behavior, I suppose. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really am trying to keep a positive attitude about myself, even when I struggle. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty or ashamed about how your body looks after one of these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I think... well, it's a lot like what I was just saying a moment ago. I don't really feel ashamed of how my body actually looks after it happens. \n\nThe guilt is really just about the... the fact that I couldn't stop eating. I don't go look in the mirror and hate my figure or anything like that. I really do try to be gentle with myself when it comes to my appearance, and just be grateful that I'm healthy enough to get around and do my volunteer work. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have that specific issue? I know a lot of women probably do struggle with that, but my shame is just about the food itself. Am I... am I answering your questions the right way? It's almost like I'm in this terrible hurry, even though I have absolutely nowhere I need to be. It's so silly, isn't it? I know I should sit down and use a plate properly like a normal person. I really am trying to work on that. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control happen at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose they do. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just shoveling food in, because I really do try to have good manners, even when I'm alone. But... I suppose I do catch myself eating faster than I normally would. 

Like, sometimes I'll make a nice casserole or bake some muffins, and... well, I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, and before I know it, I'm taking bite after bite, almost like I'm in a rush. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... yes, mostly alone. I mean, I wouldn't ever want anyone to see me eating like that, you know? It's just... it's not very ladylike, I suppose. 

I usually wait until my husband is at work or running errands. Or if he's home, I'll wait until he's busy in the garage or out in the yard. I don't want to make him uncomfortable, and I certainly don't want him to think less of me. 

So I just... I keep it to myself. It's just easier that way, for everyone. I hope that doesn't make me sound sneaky or anything! I just try to be considerate of others. Is that a common thing for people to do? I just want to be sure I'm doing things the right way... well, as right as I can be in this situation, anyway.

I really do try to keep an eye on it, you know? I don't want to let myself go. I hope that's alright? I mean, I know with the... the eating things I mentioned, you might expect a big change, but I really have managed to keep it mostly steady. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself after these episodes of eating when you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... yes. I do. I try not to be too hard on myself, because I know that's not healthy, but... it's hard not to feel just awful afterward. \n\nI'll look at the empty pan and just think about how I've wasted food that I really meant to share with others, you know? And I just feel so ashamed. I sit there and think, I'm forty-eight years old, I'm far too old to be acting this way. I should know better by now. I always promise myself it won't happen again, and I really mean it when I say it. I just want to do better. Does that make sense?
